                               Vocabulary and Formulas Appendix

                Reader-facing terminology, calculation register, and symbol index

Purpose and scope

        This appendix is a reader-facing reference for terminology, variables, and formulas used

across Phases 1–6 of the project “Genomic and Molecular Analysis of a 17p13.3 Microdeletion

and Its Candidate Visual-Neurological Mechanisms.” It preserves the frozen project’s

terminology and inferential boundaries. Definitions describe how a term is used in this project,

which may be narrower than a general textbook definition.

Formula classification rule

        Formulas are labeled as structural calculations, statistical definitions, biochemical

reactions, operational definitions, conceptual models, or Phase 5 modeling constructs. A

conceptual equation is not a validated physiological law. An ordinal score is not a biological

effect size or probability. Where the frozen record named a standard statistical method but did

not preserve its explicit equation, this appendix supplies the canonical mathematical definition

and labels it as an explanatory addition rather than silently attributing it to the original

calculation pipeline.

Fixed structural reference

        GRCh37/hg19 chr17:1,411,408–1,841,103; nominal span 429,696 bp; heterozygous

deletion. The frozen locus set contains 13 protein-coding and 4 noncoding loci. INPP5K and

RTN4RL1 are boundary-intersected. GRCh38 may be used for modern annotation only with

documented build conversion and must not silently replace the frozen coordinates.
                                                                                                           267

Appendix A — Vocabulary

          This glossary contains 123 project terms. It emphasizes terms that affect interpretation,

reproducibility, genotype-state equivalence, expression analysis, visual physiology, and causal

claims.

Term                                Project-specific description            Primary phase(s)


Adaptation                          Adjustment of retinal or neural         Phase 4; retained in Phase 6

                                    responsiveness during or after

                                    sustained or repeated stimulation. In

                                    this project, dark adaptation

                                    specifically means recovery of visual

                                    sensitivity after photobleaching.


Allele                              One of the copies or sequence states    Phases 1, 4

                                    of a locus. For this CNV,

                                    interpretation generally concerns the

                                    affected allele versus the

                                    homologous chromosome carrying

                                    the remaining copy.


Amino-acid-coding sequence          The CDS nucleotides that encode         Phase 1

                                    amino acids, excluding the stop

                                    codon when the project reports the

                                    INPP5K coding-loss percentage.


Annotation release                  A defined version of a genome/gene      Phases 1, 3, 5

                                    annotation resource. The project

                                    records releases because gene

                                    boundaries, transcripts, and labels
                                                                                        268


                                  can change without the underlying

                                  CNV changing.


Autolysosomal reformation (ALR)   Membrane-recycling process that         Phases 2, 4

                                  regenerates lysosomes from

                                  autolysosomes after prolonged

                                  autophagy. INPP5K is implicated in

                                  normal ALR.


BED coordinate convention         A 0-based, half-open interval           Phase 1

                                  convention commonly used by

                                  BED/BEDTools. The project warns

                                  that GFF3 coordinates must be

                                  converted correctly before overlap

                                  calculations.


Biallelic loss                    Disruption of both gene copies. It is   Phases 4, 5

                                  not genotype-equivalent to the one-

                                  copy heterozygous loss produced by

                                  the index CNV.


Biological replicate              An independently derived biological Phases 5, 6

                                  sample, donor, line, or clone that

                                  supports inference beyond technical

                                  repeat measurements. Many cells

                                  from one clone do not replace

                                  independent biological replication.


Boundary-intersected gene         A gene crossed by a reported CNV        Phase 1

                                  boundary rather than fully

                                  encompassed by the interval.
                                                                              269


                     INPP5K and RTN4RL1 are the two

                     boundary-intersected protein-coding

                     loci in the frozen map.


Breakpoint           The location at which a structural      Phases 1, 6

                     variant transitions between retained

                     and deleted sequence. The array-

                     reported coordinates are operational

                     boundaries, not nucleotide-exact

                     junction sequences.


Bulk RNA-seq         RNA sequencing performed on             Phase 3

                     mixed populations of cells from a

                     tissue. It measures aggregate

                     expression but cannot by itself

                     identify which constituent cell types

                     produce the signal.


Candidate modifier   A factor plausibly capable of           Phase 4

                     changing phenotype severity,

                     threshold, penetrance, or expression

                     without being established as a

                     sufficient primary cause.


Causal chain         The ordered evidentiary/biological      Phases 4, 5, 6

                     sequence from genomic disruption

                     through dosage, molecular

                     perturbation, spatial opportunity,

                     cellular physiology, systems

                     physiology, and phenotype. Missing
                                                                                    270


                            downstream links cannot be supplied

                            by strong upstream evidence.


CCDS                        Consensus Coding Sequence. In             Phase 1

                            Phase 1 it is used as a standardized

                            coding reference, including for the

                            RTN4RL1 coding-overlap

                            calculation.


Cell-type specificity       The degree to which expression or         Phases 3, 5

                            another molecular signal is

                            concentrated in one cell class relative

                            to others. It is evidence of biological

                            opportunity, not evidence of

                            dysfunction.


Cellular phenotype          A measurable change in cell               Phase 4

                            structure, survival, trafficking,

                            signaling, electrophysiology, or

                            stress response after perturbation.


CNV (copy-number variant)   A genomic deletion or duplication         All phases

                            that alters the number of copies of

                            one or more loci. The index CNV is

                            a heterozygous deletion.


Coding sequence (CDS)       The nucleotide sequence of a              Phase 1

                            transcript that encodes protein. The

                            Phase 1 overlap calculations operate

                            on CDS segments rather than total
                                                                                        271


                                     genomic gene span.


Coexpression                         Correlated or overlapping expression Phases 3, 5

                                     of genes across samples, cells, or

                                     biological units. Coexpression does

                                     not establish molecular interaction,

                                     epistasis, synergy, or causality.


Compensation                         Biological buffering that reduces the Phases 4–6

                                     functional impact of a genomic

                                     perturbation, including intact-allele

                                     expression, paralogs, developmental

                                     adaptation, or homeostatic responses.


Complete knockout                    Experimental removal or inactivation Phases 4, 5

                                     of all functional copies of a gene.

                                     Knockout evidence is not treated as

                                     equivalent to heterozygous deletion

                                     evidence.


Confidence interval / uncertainty    A quantitative interval representing     Phase 5

interval                             uncertainty around an estimate.

                                     Phase 5 requires uncertainty to be

                                     retained where calculable rather than

                                     replaced by point estimates alone.


Constraint / dosage characteristic   Genomic or gene-level evidence           Phase 5

                                     relevant to tolerance of loss or copy-

                                     number change. In Phase 5, such

                                     characteristics may be used in
                                                                                   272


                           matched null intervals when feasible.


Counterevidence            Evidence that contradicts or weakens Phases 4–6

                           a proposed mechanism, such as

                           normal heterozygous phenotypes,

                           compensation, reduced rather than

                           increased physiological response, or

                           protective effects under specific

                           conditions.


Cryptographic hash         A deterministic digital fingerprint       Phase 5

                           proposed for freezing Phase 5 inputs

                           so later users can verify that the

                           analyzed dataset has not silently

                           changed.


Dark adaptation            Recovery of visual sensitivity after      Phases 4–6

                           photobleaching or sustained light

                           exposure; treated as a possible retinal

                           reserve endpoint.


Detection fraction         The fraction of cells/nuclei or           Phases 3, 5

                           samples in which a gene is

                           detectably measured. In single-cell

                           data it is influenced by both biology

                           and technical dropout.


Developmental trajectory   The pattern of a gene’s expression or Phase 3

                           biological effect across

                           developmental stages. Phase 3

                           specifies within-gene normalization
                                                                                                  273


                                    to identify relative prenatal, neonatal,

                                    childhood, or adult peaks.


Directional convergence             Multiple perturbations pushing a           Phase 4

                                    shared endpoint in the same

                                    direction. This is stronger than

                                    merely affecting the same system,

                                    but still does not itself prove

                                    synergy.


dLGN / lateral geniculate nucleus   The dorsal lateral geniculate nucleus, Phases 3–6

(LGN)                               the principal thalamic relay between

                                    retinal ganglion cells and primary

                                    visual cortex.


Dominant-negative variant           A variant whose product interferes         Phases 4, 5

                                    with the normal product. Its

                                    mechanism is not assumed to be

                                    equivalent to a simple heterozygous

                                    deletion.


Dosage consequence                  A demonstrated change in RNA,              Phases 4–6

                                    protein, molecular function, or

                                    cellular behavior attributable to

                                    copy-number/genotype state.

                                    Deletion alone does not establish the

                                    size of the dosage effect.


Dosage sensitivity                  The degree to which changing gene          Phases 1, 2, 4–6

                                    copy number alters transcript,

                                    protein, molecular function, cell
                                                                                    274


                            behavior, or phenotype.


Dropout                     Failure to detect a transcript in       Phase 3

                            single-cell/single-nucleus sequencing

                            even when biologically present,

                            especially for low-abundance

                            transcripts. Zero counts in a small

                            cell population are therefore not

                            automatically biological absence.


Efferocytosis               Recognition and engulfment of           Phases 2, 4

                            apoptotic cells or debris by

                            phagocytic cells; SCARF1

                            participates in C1q-associated

                            efferocytosis.


Electroretinography (ERG)   Measurement of retinal electrical       Phases 4, 6

                            responses to light. The a-wave

                            primarily reflects photoreceptor

                            activity; the b-wave reflects

                            downstream inner-retinal activity.


Endolysosomal system        The connected endosome-to-              Phases 2, 4–6

                            lysosome network that sorts,

                            transports, degrades, and recycles

                            cellular cargo.


Enrichment                  A signal occurring more strongly or     Phases 3, 5

                            frequently than a defined comparison

                            or null expectation. Phase 5 requires

                            matched null analysis before treating
                                                                                              275


                                     convergence as unusually specific.


Entrapment of missingness / missing Project rule that an unmeasured           Phase 5

≠ zero                               causal layer must remain

                                     missing/unresolved rather than being

                                     encoded as zero or inferred from

                                     neighboring layers. Zero is reserved

                                     for defined null or evidence-of-

                                     absence states.


Epileptogenesis                      The biological process by which a        Phases 4–6

                                     network develops a persistent

                                     tendency to generate seizures.

                                     MIR22 evidence in the project is

                                     most directly related to post-insult

                                     epileptogenesis rather than photic

                                     seizure causation.


Epileptogenic reserve                Conceptual resistance of a neural        Phases 4–6

                                     network to transitions into persistent

                                     or recurrent epileptic activity; lower

                                     reserve implies easier pathological

                                     transition under challenge.


Epistasis                            A genetic interaction in which the       Phases 2, 4–6

                                     effect of one locus depends on the

                                     state of another. The project did not

                                     demonstrate epistasis among deleted

                                     loci.


Evidence hierarchy                   The ordered project rule: genomic        Phases 4–6

                             evidence → molecular mechanism

                             → expression/spatiotemporal

                             opportunity → perturbational

                             evidence → cellular phenotype →

                             phenotype association → causality.


Expression prevalence        The proportion of relevant biological Phase 5

                             units showing detectable expression;

                             closely related to detection fraction

                             and distinct from expression

                             magnitude.


False discovery rate (FDR)   Expected proportion of false            Phase 3

                             discoveries among findings declared

                             significant. Phase 3 specifies

                             Benjamini-Hochberg control with q

                             testing.


Functional convergence       Independent genes or mechanisms         Phases 2–6

                             affecting the same molecular system,

                             cellular process, anatomical

                             compartment, developmental

                             operation, or phenotype endpoint.


Functional reserve           Difference between a system’s           Phases 4–6

                             available/maximal biological

                             capacity and the physiological
                                                                                    277


                             demand placed on that system. The

                             project’s leading model proposes

                             distributed reduction or alteration of

                             reserve rather than obligatory

                             baseline failure.


Gain                         Ratio between change in output and       Phase 4

                             change in input. Increased sensory

                             gain means a larger physiological

                             response to the same matched

                             stimulus.


Gain-of-function variant     A variant that increases or changes      Phases 4, 5

                             activity in a way not equivalent to

                             simple loss of one copy.


Genome assembly              A defined coordinate reference for       All phases

                             the genome. The frozen structural

                             reference is GRCh37/hg19; GRCh38

                             may be used for modern annotation

                             only with documented conversion.


Genotype-state discipline    Project rule requiring explicit          Phases 4–6

                             distinction among heterozygous

GFF3 coordinate convention   A 1-based, inclusive annotation          Phase 1

                          coordinate convention used in the

                          workflow.


Haploinsufficiency        A state in which one functional gene Phases 2, 4–6

                          copy is insufficient for normal

                          molecular, cellular, or clinical

                          function. It must be demonstrated

                          rather than presumed from deletion.


Heterozygous deletion /   Loss or disruption of one of two        All phases

heterozygous loss         gene copies. It does not

                          automatically imply exactly 50%

Hypomorph                 An allele retaining partial gene        Phases 4, 5

                          function rather than complete loss.


In silico                 Analysis conducted computationally      Phases 5, 6

                          using genomic, transcriptomic,

Independent replication   Reproduction of an observation in       Phases 3, 5, 6

                          independent donors, lines, datasets,

                          studies, or experiments rather than

                          repeated measurements of the same

                          underlying sample.


Isogenic                  Genetically matched experimental        Phases 4, 6
                                                                                           279


                                   lines differing only at the engineered

(M), parvocellular (P)             visual-thalamic organization. Phase 3

Leave-one-locus-out analysis       Sensitivity procedure in which each       Phase 5

                                   affected locus is removed in turn and

Leave-one-source-out analysis      Sensitivity procedure that removes        Phase 5

                            frozen deletion affects at least 17

Marker gene                 A gene reproducibly enriched in or        Phase 3

                            characteristic of a cell class/subtype.

Mechanistic domain          A project-defined biological              Phase 5

                            biological evidence but not

                              miR-22 abundance.


Monte Carlo simulation        Repeated stochastic sampling used in Phase 5

                              Phase 5 to examine methodological

Multiple-testing correction   Adjustment for testing many             Phases 3, 5, 6

                              hypotheses/frequencies so nominally

                                                                                        282


                                permit within-dataset comparison.

nTPM                            Normalized transcripts per million as Phase 3

                                resources used in Phase 3 screening.

Null / weak-specificity model   Competing model in which the              Phases 5, 6

                                deleted genes have real biology but

                                the apparent visual-neurological

                                convergence is not specific evidence

                                for the index phenotype.


Null result                     A measured result that does not           All phases

                                support the tested effect under

                                specified conditions. It is retained as

                                evidence rather than discarded or

                                redefined.


Operational CNV boundary        The reported array-coordinate edge        Phases 1, 6

                                used for calculations. It is not

Outer segment                   Specialized photoreceptor                 Phases 4, 6

                                compartment containing
                                                                                283


                        photopigment-rich membranes where

Pairwise coexpression   Coexpression evaluated between two Phase 5

                        genes, typically across independent

                        biological units. It is distinct from

Paralog compensation    Functional buffering by a related       Phases 4–6

                        gene with overlapping function. It is

                        a potential reason why severe

                        knockout phenotypes may not appear

                        after one-copy loss.


Parameter sensitivity   Dependence of a model conclusion        Phase 5

                        on defensible changes to weights,

                        mappings, penalties, or missing-data

                        rules. A conclusion is called robust

                        only if it survives reasonable

                        variation.


Partial gene deletion   A deletion affecting only part of a     Phases 1, 5

                        gene/locus. The project treats

                              retinal trophic, antiangiogenic, lipid-

Perturbation equivalence      The degree to which an experimental Phase 5

                              heterozygous deletion as equivalent.


Phase locking                 Consistency of response phase             Phases 4, 5, 6

                              relative to a periodic stimulus or

Phenotype association         Observed statistical or clinical          Phases 4–6

                              relationship between genotype and

                              phenotype. Association is

Phosphoinositide              A phosphorylated                          Phases 2, 4–6

Photochemical vulnerability   Greater molecular or cellular injury      Phase 4

                                 sensory response.


Photoparoxysmal response (PPR)   An abnormal epileptiform EEG            Phases 4–6

Photosensitive epilepsy (PSE)    Epilepsy in which visual stimulation    Phases 4–6

                                 can provoke abnormal EEG activity

Phototransduction                Conversion of absorbed photons into Phase 4

                             WDR81/WDR91-associated system

Pseudobulk                   Aggregation of single-cell             Phase 3

Pseudogene                   A gene-like sequence generally         Phases 1, 3

Pseudoreplication            Treating non-independent               Phases 3, 5, 6

                                   inflating apparent precision.

                                   Phase 3 specifies q < 0.05 as the

Random-interval null model         Phase 5 control strategy comparing     Phase 5

                                   the index CNV with matched

                                   genomic intervals processed through

                                   pipeline.


Rescue                             Correction or restoration of a         Phases 4–6

                                   gene/CNV state or pathway that

                                   reverses a phenotype. Rescue

                                   provides stronger causal specificity

Retinal ganglion cell (RGC)        Output neuron of the retina whose      Phases 3, 4

                                   axons form the optic nerve and

                                  including photoreceptor survival,

                                  that remains stable across reasonable

                                  analyses.


Sensory hypersensitivity          An abnormally large perceptual or        Phase 4

                                  physiological response to the same

                                  light stimulus. This is distinct from

Sequential-process convergence    Different genes acting at distinct       Phase 5

                                  stages of the same biological

                                  process. Phase 5 distinguishes this

                                  synergy.

                                  It provides cell-type localization but

seq)                              Useful for human brain tissue but

                                       in transcript capture and abundance

Spatial co-localization                Presence of relevant                     Phases 3, 5

                                       genes/mechanisms in the same tissue

                                       or cell type, providing opportunity

Spatiotemporal opportunity             Evidence that a gene/mechanism is        Phases 3–6

                                       expressed in the relevant tissue/cell

                                       type and developmental or

                                       physiological window. Expression

                                       creates opportunity, not dysfunction.


Spearman rank correlation              Nonparametric rank-based measure         Phase 3

                                       specified in Phase 3 for coexpression

Spliceosome                            Large ribonucleoprotein machinery        Phases 2, 4

                                       that removes introns from pre-

                                       mRNA; PRPF8 is a central U5

                                       spliceosomal scaffold.

(SSVEP)                                response produced by periodic visual

Stress-dependent vulnerability         A phenotype that may be                  Phases 4–6

                                       weak/absent at baseline but emerges
                                                                                          290


                                  under oxidative stress, injury,

                                  repeated activity, aging, metabolic

                                  demand, or other challenge.


Synergy                           A combined effect greater than          Phases 2, 4–6

                                  expected under a specified additive

                                  model. The project does not claim

                                  demonstrated synergy among deleted

                                  loci.


Temporal functional convergence   Different loci affecting the same       Phase 4

                                  endpoint at different developmental

                                  or physiological stages rather than

                                  acting simultaneously.


Topological convergence           Different perturbations affecting the   Phase 4

                                  same biological network or structural

                                  endpoint even if they push that

                                  endpoint in different directions.


TPM (transcripts per million)     RNA-seq abundance normalization         Phase 3

                                  used in Phase 3 tissue-expression

                                  resources. Cross-platform absolute

                                  TPM values are not assumed directly

                                  comparable.


Visual-neural reserve             Integrated capacity of retinal, visual- Phases 4–6

                                  pathway, and cortical systems to

                                  maintain stable function under

                                  physiological or pathological
                                                                                                 291


                                 demand.


Wet-lab validation               Direct experimental testing in          Phase 6

                                 engineered/biological systems. Phase

                                 6 proposes exact-CNV isogenic

                                 systems with rescue/correction rather

                                 than inferring exact-genotype

                                 physiology from literature alone.


Within-gene z-score              A standardized value proposed in        Phase 3

                                 Phase 3 to show whether a gene is

                                 relatively high or low compared with

                                 its own expression across

                                 developmental stages, not to

                                 compare absolute expression

                                 between different genes.


Appendix B — Formula and Calculation Register

       This register contains 45 formulas, reactions, quantitative definitions, and model

expressions. Each entry states what the expression means and whether it was an actual project

calculation, a specified analysis method, or a conceptual/future-test model.

B.1 Nominal interval span

       L = D_e - D_s + 1

       Variables / definitions: D_s = reported inclusive start coordinate; D_e = reported

inclusive end coordinate.

       Interpretation: For chr17:1,411,408–1,841,103, this yields 429,696 bp.

       Status / limitation: Structural calculation; exact for the reported inclusive coordinates, not

the physical junction length.
                                                                                              292

       Project provenance: Phase 1

B.2 Per-CDS-segment overlap

       L_overlap = max(0, min(C_e, D_e) - max(C_s, D_s) + 1)

       Variables / definitions: C_s/C_e = inclusive CDS-segment start/end; D_s/D_e = inclusive

deletion start/end.

       Interpretation: Counts the number of deleted bases within one CDS segment under 1-

based inclusive coordinates.

       Status / limitation: Structural calculation used by the replication-ready Phase 1 method.

       Project provenance: Phase 1

B.3 Total deleted coding length

       L_deleted = Σ_i L_overlap,i

       Variables / definitions: i indexes CDS segments.

       Interpretation: Sums deleted bases across all CDS segments of the reference transcript.

       Status / limitation: Structural calculation.

       Project provenance: Phase 1

B.4 Total CDS length

       L_CDS = Σ_i (C_e,i - C_s,i + 1)

       Variables / definitions: i indexes CDS segments.

       Interpretation: Total coding length of the transcript under inclusive coordinates.

       Status / limitation: Structural calculation.

       Project provenance: Phase 1

B.5 Percent CDS deleted

       %CDS deleted = (L_deleted / L_CDS) × 100
                                                                                                293

       Variables / definitions: L_deleted = deleted coding bases; L_CDS = total coding bases.

       Interpretation: Quantifies the fraction of coding sequence intersected by the CNV. Stop-

codon handling must be stated.

       Status / limitation: Structural calculation.

       Project provenance: Phase 1

B.6 INPP5K amino-acid coding denominator

448 aa × 3 nt/aa = 1,344 coding nt

       Variables / definitions: 448 = protein length used in Phase 1; stop codon excluded for

amino-acid-coding calculation.

       Interpretation: Provides the denominator for the INPP5K amino-acid-coding loss

estimate.

       Status / limitation: Worked structural calculation.

       Project provenance: Phase 1

B.7 INPP5K coding-loss estimate

666 / 1,344 = 0.4955 ≈ 49.6%

       Variables / definitions: 666 = approximate deleted coding nucleotides under the reported-

coordinate model.

       Interpretation: Approximate fraction of MANE amino-acid-coding sequence lying within

the deleted interval on the affected allele.

       Status / limitation: Worked structural estimate; dependent on reported CNV boundaries

and transcript model.

       Project provenance: Phase 1
                                                                                                294

B.8 RTN4RL1 CCDS-loss estimate

       1,313 / 1,326 × 100 ≈ 99.0%

       Variables / definitions: 1,313 = approximate CCDS nucleotides on deleted side; 1,326 =

total CCDS nucleotide count stated in Phase 1.

       Interpretation: Shows that small genomic-span intersection can correspond to near-total

coding disruption.

       Status / limitation: Worked structural estimate; coordinate-model dependent.

       Project provenance: Phase 1

B.9 BED conversion for an inclusive deletion interval

       BED_start = D_s - 1; BED_end = D_e

       Variables / definitions: D_s/D_e are 1-based inclusive coordinates.

       Interpretation: Converts the deletion to 0-based half-open BED form; Phase 1 gives chr17

1411407 1841103.

       Status / limitation: Coordinate-system conversion rule.

       Project provenance: Phase 1

B.10 Developmental within-gene z-score (canonical definition)

       z_g,t = (x_g,t - mean_g) / SD_g

       Variables / definitions: x_g,t = expression for gene g at stage t; mean_g and SD_g are

calculated across the stages included for that gene.

       Interpretation: Implements the Phase 3 concept of expressing each developmental value

relative to that gene’s own trajectory.
                                                                                                295

       Status / limitation: Canonical mathematical definition supplied here for reader clarity; the

frozen Phase 3 text specifies a within-gene z-score but does not preserve an explicit equation or

exact preprocessing pipeline.

       Project provenance: Phase 3

B.11 Detection fraction

       p_detect = n_detected / n_total

       Variables / definitions: n_detected = cells/nuclei or biological units passing the defined

detection threshold; n_total = units tested.

       Interpretation: Quantifies expression prevalence. Threshold and unit of independence

must be declared.

       Status / limitation: Canonical implementation of the Phase 3/5 metric; threshold was

dataset-dependent.

       Project provenance: Phases 3, 5

B.12 Spearman rank correlation

       ρ_s = corr(rank(X), rank(Y))

       Variables / definitions: X, Y = expression values across independent biological units.

       Interpretation: Rank-based coexpression measure specified because linearity is not

assumed.

       Status / limitation: Statistical method specified in Phase 3; exact handling of ties/missing

data must follow the software implementation.

       Project provenance: Phase 3
                                                                                                  296

B.13 Benjamini-Hochberg FDR adjustment

         Sort p_(1) ≤ ... ≤ p_(m); compare p_(i) to (i/m)Q, or compute adjusted q-values

monotonically from m·p_(i)/i

         Variables / definitions: m = number of tests; i = ordered test rank; Q = target FDR (Phase

3 primary criterion q < 0.05).

         Interpretation: Controls expected false-discovery proportion across multiple coexpression

tests.

         Status / limitation: Canonical algorithmic definition supplied for reader clarity; project

specifies BH-FDR, not a custom implementation.

         Project provenance: Phase 3

B.14 INPP5K 5-phosphatase reaction

         PI(4,5)P2 + H2O → PI4P + Pi

         Variables / definitions: Pi = inorganic phosphate.

         Interpretation: Represents established INPP5K 5-phosphatase chemistry.

         Status / limitation: Biochemical reaction; not a quantitative in vivo flux model.

         Project provenance: Phase 4

B.15 INPP5K PIP3 reaction

         PI(3,4,5)P3 + H2O → PI(3,4)P2 + Pi

         Variables / definitions: Substrate/product phosphoinositides; Pi = inorganic phosphate.

         Interpretation: Represents an established substrate reaction of INPP5K.

         Status / limitation: Biochemical reaction; do not infer in vivo flux from kinetic constants

alone.

         Project provenance: Phase 4
                                                                                            297

B.16 VPS34 reaction

       PI + ATP → PI3P + ADP

       Variables / definitions: PI = phosphatidylinositol.

       Interpretation: Represents kinase chemistry associated with endosomal PI3P production.

       Status / limitation: Biochemical reaction.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.17 PLC branch

       PI(4,5)P2 → DAG + IP3

       Variables / definitions: DAG = diacylglycerol; IP3 = inositol trisphosphate.

       Interpretation: Canonical signaling branch relevant to calcium-regulatory context.

       Status / limitation: Biochemical pathway representation.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.18 Dynein/ATPase work

       ATP + H2O → ADP + Pi + mechanical work

       Variables / definitions: Pi = inorganic phosphate.

       Interpretation: Represents ATP-powered motor/remodeling work; RILP recruits dynein

but does not catalyze ATP hydrolysis itself.

       Status / limitation: Biochemical/energetic representation.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.19 PEDF-R/PNPLA2 phospholipase representation

       phospholipid + H2O → lysophospholipid + free fatty acid

       Variables / definitions: Generic phospholipid substrate/products.

       Interpretation: Simplified representation of phospholipase/lipase chemistry.
                                                                                           298

       Status / limitation: Biochemical simplification, not a complete enzyme mechanism.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.20 RPA binding equilibrium

       ssDNA + RPA ⇌ RPA-coated ssDNA

       Variables / definitions: ssDNA = single-stranded DNA.

       Interpretation: Binding equilibrium underlying RPA-mediated ATR recruitment and

DNA-damage signaling.

       Status / limitation: Conceptual biochemical equilibrium.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.21 Rhodopsin conformational equilibrium

       MI ⇌ MII

       Variables / definitions: MI/MII = metarhodopsin I/II states.

       Interpretation: Simplified photopigment conformational equilibrium influenced by

membrane environment.

       Status / limitation: Biochemical/biophysical representation.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.22 Lipid-radical initiation

       LH + radical → L•

       Variables / definitions: LH = lipid; L• = lipid radical.

       Interpretation: Simplified initiation step in lipid peroxidation.

       Status / limitation: Biochemical representation.

       Project provenance: Phase 4
                                                                                            299

B.23 Lipid-peroxyl propagation

       L• + O2 → LOO•

       Variables / definitions: L• = lipid radical; LOO• = lipid peroxyl radical.

       Interpretation: Simplified oxygen-addition step in lipid peroxidation.

       Status / limitation: Biochemical representation.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.24 Methionine activation

       Met + ATP → SAM

       Variables / definitions: Met = methionine; SAM = S-adenosylmethionine.

       Interpretation: Simplified activation route generating methyl donor SAM.

       Status / limitation: Biochemical representation.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.25 SAM-dependent lysine methylation

       Lys + SAM → methyl-Lys + SAH

       Variables / definitions: SAH = S-adenosylhomocysteine.

       Interpretation: Simplified methyltransferase reaction relevant to SMYD4.

       Status / limitation: Biochemical representation.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.26 Steady-state flux balance

       dX/dt = J_in - J_out

       Variables / definitions: X = pool/concentration; J_in/J_out = input/output fluxes.

       Interpretation: At steady state dX/dt = 0 even when turnover is high; normal

concentration does not prove normal flux.
                                                                                                300

       Status / limitation: Quantitative definition/conceptual mass balance.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.27 Functional-reserve condition

       phenotype risk rises when D(t) > C_max

       Variables / definitions: D(t) = physiological demand at time t; C_max = available system

capacity.

       Interpretation: Formalizes the reserve concept: phenotype may emerge when demand

exceeds capacity.

       Status / limitation: Conceptual model; not a calibrated probability law.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.28 Light-injury function

       I_CNV(H) > I_WT(H) at matched H indicates greater vulnerability

       Variables / definitions: I(H) = measured injury; H = light/photon exposure.

       Interpretation: A direct vulnerability phenotype requires greater injury in CNV versus

WT at the same exposure.

       Status / limitation: Operational definition for future testing.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.29 Light-injury threshold

       H_crit = exposure producing a predefined injury criterion; vulnerability if H_crit,CNV <

H_crit,WT

       Variables / definitions: H_crit = challenge dose at a prespecified injury endpoint.

       Interpretation: Lower threshold in CNV would support greater photochemical

vulnerability.
                                                                                                    301

         Status / limitation: Operational definition.

         Project provenance: Phase 4

B.30 Neural response gain

         G = Δoutput / Δinput

         Variables / definitions: Input/output must be explicitly defined and measured under

matched conditions.

         Interpretation: Sensory/neural amplification requires G_CNV > G_WT for the same input

domain.

         Status / limitation: Operational quantitative definition.

         Project provenance: Phase 4

B.31 Retinal temporal gain

         G_R(f) = |R(f)| / |S(f)|

         Variables / definitions: R(f) = retinal output amplitude at frequency f; S(f) = stimulus

amplitude at f.

         Interpretation: Quantifies frequency-dependent retinal transfer gain.

         Status / limitation: Operational quantitative definition for future flicker testing.

         Project provenance: Phase 4

B.32 Serial bottleneck capacity

         J_max ≈ min(C1, C2, …, Cn)

         Variables / definitions: C_i = capacity of sequential processing stage i.

         Interpretation: Represents a serial process whose throughput is limited by the weakest

stage.

         Status / limitation: Conceptual model; approximation.
                                                                                                   302

           Project provenance: Phase 4

B.33 Layered lesion burden

           dL/dt = D - P - R

           Variables / definitions: L = lesion/damage burden; D = damage generation; P =

prevention/tolerance; R = repair capacity.

           Interpretation: Qualitative reserve representation balancing damage and

protective/reparative processes.

           Status / limitation: Conceptual model; not a validated physiological differential equation.

           Project provenance: Phase 4

B.34 Factorial interaction model

           Y = β0 + βA·A + βB·B + βAB·(A×B) + ε

           Variables / definitions: Y = outcome; A/B = perturbation indicators or doses; βAB =

interaction coefficient; ε = residual.

           Interpretation: βAB tests departure from the specified additive model; useful for testing

non-additive interaction/synergy claims.

           Status / limitation: Standard statistical model proposed for reproducible interaction

testing.

           Project provenance: Phase 4

B.35 Developmental timing model

           P = f(D_early, D_late, D_adult)

           Variables / definitions: P = mature phenotype; D terms = dosage effects during

developmental windows.
                                                                                              303

       Interpretation: Decomposes mature phenotype conceptually into early, late, and adult

contributions.

       Status / limitation: Conceptual model.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.36 Photosensitive-network threshold

       G_N × I > Θ_E

       Variables / definitions: I = visual input; G_N = network propagation gain; Θ_E =

threshold for pathological synchronization.

       Interpretation: Conceptual condition under which visually driven network activity could

cross a pathological threshold.

       Status / limitation: Conceptual model; not calibrated to the index CNV.

       Project provenance: Phase 4

B.37 Locus-level evidence function

       E_i,k = f(D_i, M_i, S_i, P_i, V_i, N_i, R_i, C_i)

       Variables / definitions: D_i = dosage evidence; M_i = molecular perturbation; S_i =

spatiotemporal opportunity; P_i = perturbation equivalence; V_i = visual relevance; N_i =

neural/network relevance; R_i = rescue/causal specificity; C_i = contradiction penalty.

       Interpretation: Defines support from locus i to mechanistic domain k without assuming

simple additivity.

       Status / limitation: Phase 5 modeling framework; f was intentionally not fixed to a

biologically unjustified numerical law.

       Project provenance: Phase 5
                                                                                                304

B.38 Domain-level evidence aggregation

         F_k = g(E_1,k, E_2,k, …, E_n,k)

         Variables / definitions: E_i,k = locus-level contributions to domain k.

         Interpretation: Aggregates locus evidence while requiring attention to correlated evidence

and double-counting.

         Status / limitation: Phase 5 modeling framework; g may be weighted, gated, minimum-

link, geometric, or other justified form.

         Project provenance: Phase 5

B.39 Causal-layer sequence

         G → D → M → S → F_R → F_N → F_C → F_P

         Variables / definitions: G = genomic disruption; D = dosage; M = molecular/cellular

perturbation; S = spatiotemporal opportunity; F_R = retinal consequence; F_N = neural/circuit

consequence; F_C = cortical/network transformation; F_P = photic phenotype relevance.

         Interpretation: Represents evidence propagation through necessary biological layers.

         Status / limitation: Phase 5 causal architecture.

         Project provenance: Phase 5

B.40 Bottleneck score example

         B = min(G, D, M, S, F_R, F_N, F_C, F_P)

         Variables / definitions: Terms are causal-layer support measures on a common defined

scale.

         Interpretation: Prevents strong early-layer evidence from compensating numerically for

an absent necessary downstream layer.
                                                                                                305

       Status / limitation: Candidate Phase 5 formulation; explicitly not to be adopted

automatically.

       Project provenance: Phase 5

B.41 Convergence matrix

       K_ij

       Variables / definitions: K_ij records evidence that loci i and j converge biologically and

the reason for the edge.

       Interpretation: Encodes pairwise convergence while distinguishing pathway co-

membership, direct interaction, sequential dependency, and demonstrated interaction.

       Status / limitation: Phase 5 model object; not inherently a causal or synergy matrix.

       Project provenance: Phase 5

B.42 Directional consistency coding

       direction ∈ {+1, 0, −1}

       Variables / definitions: +1 = supports proposed mechanism; 0 = neutral/noninformative;

−1 = contradicts.

       Interpretation: Prevents counting any phenotype as supportive merely because a

perturbation produces an effect.

       Status / limitation: Phase 5 deterministic coding rule.

       Project provenance: Phase 5

B.43 Ordinal evidence example

       0=no relevant evidence; 1=weak/indirect; 2=moderate; 3=strong/direct;

4=replicated/direct/rescue-supported

       Variables / definitions: Scale is illustrative unless formally frozen in the implementation.
                                                                                                  306

        Interpretation: Provides an interpretable ordinal mapping when continuous quantification

is not justified.

        Status / limitation: Phase 5 example scale; ordinal score is not a biological effect size or

probability.

        Project provenance: Phase 5

B.44 Empirical null-tail estimate (canonical)

        p_emp = (1 + #{T_null ≥ T_index}) / (N_null + 1)

        Variables / definitions: T_index = index-interval statistic; T_null = same statistic in

matched null intervals; N_null = number of null intervals.

        Interpretation: A standard way to quantify how extreme the index statistic is versus a

simulated/matched null distribution.

        Status / limitation: Canonical formula included for reader clarity because Phase 5 calls

for empirical enrichment statistics but the accessible frozen protocol does not preserve a unique

implemented formula.

        Project provenance: Phase 5

B.45 Monte Carlo standard error for an estimated probability (canonical)

        MCSE(p̂ ) = sqrt[p̂ (1-p̂ )/N]

        Variables / definitions: p̂ = simulated proportion; N = number of Monte Carlo replicates.

        Interpretation: Shows why 20,000 replicates per scenario/cell can make stochastic

sampling error small relative to biological uncertainty.

        Status / limitation: Canonical simulation formula supplied for reader clarity; the Phase 5

summary reports replicate counts but not a stored MCSE equation.

        Project provenance: Phase 5
                                                                                               307

Appendix D — Interpretation and Reproducibility Rules

         Genomic deletion does not by itself establish RNA, protein, or functional dosage.

         Expression establishes biological opportunity, not dysfunction or causal effect.

         Knockout, biallelic loss, hypomorph, pathogenic missense/dominant-negative/gain-of-

function states, and pharmacologic inhibition are not interchangeable with heterozygous deletion.

         Coexpression, pathway membership, network proximity, sequential dependence,

biochemical interaction, genetic interaction, epistasis, and synergy are distinct evidence classes.

         An ordinal evidence score is not a biological effect size, probability, or percent causality.

         Missing evidence must remain missing. It must not be recoded as zero merely to

complete a matrix.

         Zero should represent a defined null result or evidence of absence under stated

conditions.

         Conceptual equations require independent calibration data before they can be interpreted

quantitatively.
                                                                                                    312

          Frequency scans and high-dimensional tests require prespecified correction for multiple

comparisons.

          Cells from the same clone/donor are not independent biological replicates unless the

experimental design explicitly supports that assumption.

          Null findings, contradictory effects, and failed hypotheses remain part of the frozen

record.

          GRCh37/hg19 remains the controlling CNV coordinate system unless the project is

formally versioned; GRCh38 annotations require documented conversion and cannot silently

replace the fixed interval.

          The project’s strongest current interpretation is a candidate distributed visual-neural

functional-reserve lesion; direct causation of PPR or visually triggered seizures by the exact

heterozygous CNV remains unresolved.

Appendix E — Provenance Note

          This appendix was compiled from the frozen Phase 1 structural report, Phase 2 molecular

synthesis, Phase 3 expression/spatiotemporal research records, Phase 4 frozen master report, the

Phase 5 deterministic modeling protocol and frozen summary available in the project record, and

the Phase 6 frozen conclusion. It does not reopen prior conclusions. Where a canonical formula

was added because the frozen record named a standard method but did not print its equation (for

example Spearman correlation, Benjamini-Hochberg correction, within-gene z-score, empirical

null-tail probability, or Monte Carlo standard error), the entry is explicitly labeled as an

explanatory mathematical definition rather than a recovered hidden calculation.

Bibliographic Note

This bibliography consolidates the source lists preserved across Phase 1; Phase 2; the Phase 3

       research-through-3D, abstract/methods, and integrated expression/spatiotemporal

       records; Phase 4; the available frozen Phase 5 summary; and Phase 6. Clear duplicates

       were merged by DOI, URL, or title where the identity was unambiguous. The available

       Phase 5 casual-reader summary contains no standalone Works Cited section, so no

       unique Phase 5 external reference has been invented; its scientific evidence base is

       inherited from the earlier frozen phases and the Phase 5 evidence model. Several frozen

       reports preserved author fields as “et al.” rather than complete author lists. Those

       omissions are retained rather than reconstructed from outside sources. Accordingly, the

       entries below follow APA 7 ordering, date placement, retrieval-date conventions, and

       hanging-indent layout to the extent permitted by the metadata preserved in the project

       record.
